* Two reads held behind RREADY, a third read waiting at a register block, and a write to the same block landing while that read's response waits, so the block holds two accesses
* Every AxPROT bit set and cleared on both address channels; a read and a write to an unmapped word with every address bit above the CSR map set, each behind a mapped access, answered DECERR; a write at an unaligned address with the strobe from that byte up waiting in the AW spill register; a read of every register-holding subordinate at an unaligned byte address, answered OKAY with the word that contains it
* Every CT_SRC select holding a drawn mask while the bursts run, so the held read data carries the upper select bits; no trigger is pulsed, and every select is written back to 0 at the end
* A write to the matrix and one to an unmapped word with W three cycles behind AW, and two matrix reads one cycle apart

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Idle all manual AXI-Lite master outputs after reset. Every access below goes through the master's pipelined operation, which launches each beat at its seeded delay or as soon as the beat ahead of it on its channel is accepted, and holds BREADY and RREADY for the given cycles after the first response. For every pipeline, verify each access answers DECERR exactly when no subordinate decodes its word and OKAY otherwise, every spill register's READY and VALID match the beats it holds (`xtrig_axil_spill_err_count` does not advance), and the port counters `xtrig_axil_aw_stall_count`, `xtrig_axil_w_stall_count` and `xtrig_axil_ar_stall_count` advance by the stall cycles the VIP saw on each channel.
. Write a seeded mask to every CT_SRC select in one pipeline.
. For each of the eighteen crossbar subordinates in a seeded order, issue seven reads of seeded words at seeded AxPROT values with the third to that subordinate, holding RREADY for 12..16 cycles after the first RVALID. Verify the port held ARREADY low (`xtrig_axil_ar_stall_count` advanced).
. For each subordinate in a fresh seeded order, issue seven writes of seeded words the same way, the fifth at a seeded unaligned byte with the strobe from that byte up, holding BREADY for 12..16 cycles. Verify the port held AWREADY and WREADY low.
. On a seeded CTP, issue a STRETCH_MULT write, a CONFIG read, and a second STRETCH_MULT write, with the read launched 0..2 cycles and the second write's AW 0..2 cycles after the first write and its W 0 or 2 cycles behind that AW; then, at the same offsets, a CONFIG read, a STRETCH_MULT write and a second CONFIG read. Repeat on the matrix with a write to one seeded CT_SRC select and reads of another. Verify the crossbar demux held a refused AW at least once across the sweep (`xtrig_demux_aw_lock`).
. On the same CTP and on the matrix, issue reads of two other subordinates, a read of the block and a write to it, with the write launched 6, 8, 10, 12 or 14 cycles after the start, holding RREADY for 12..16 cycles.
. Issue a CONFIG read at AxPROT 0, a read of `0xFFFF_FC00` at AxPROT 7, a STRETCH_MULT write at AxPROT 0, a write to `0xFFFF_FC00` at AxPROT 7, a CONFIG read and a STRETCH_MULT write; then one read of each register-holding subordinate at a seeded unaligned byte of a seeded word.
. Issue a CT_SRC write and an unmapped write, each with W three cycles behind AW, and two CT_SRC reads with the second AR one cycle after the first.
. Write 0 to every CT_SRC select in one pipeline.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`xtrig_outstanding_resp_chk` |Every access answers DECERR exactly when no subordinate decodes its word and OKAY otherwise, and mapped reads match the `xtrig_csr` scoreboard feature (`CHK-XTRIG-AXIL`)
|`xtrig_outstanding_port_chk` |Every spill register's READY and VALID match the beats it holds, and the port stall counters equal the VIP's stall cycles on each channel (`CHK-XTRIG-AXIL`)
|`xtrig_outstanding_stall_chk` |Each read burst drops ARREADY at the port (`CHK-XTRIG-AR-STALL`), and each write burst AWREADY and WREADY (`CHK-XTRIG-AXIL`)
|`xtrig_outstanding_aw_lock_chk` |The crossbar demux holds a refused AW at least once in the sweep (`CHK-XTRIG-AW-LOCK`)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every outstanding access completes with the response its address decodes to, the port stalls when the bursts back up, and the demux AW lock engages, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[dtp-formal-plan]]
=== Formal Verification Plan

Formal property checking corroborates the control-logic rows of the
traceability matrix; it does not replace the simulation credit. The open-path
flow runs SymbiYosys through the runner's formal mode
(`tools/dv/doc/run-dv.adoc`, Formal verification) and follows the property
style in `hw/common/dv/docs/formal-property-style.adoc`. `dtp_formal_cfg.toml`
and `testlists/formal.toml` are the launch entry; the properties, the bind
files, the task files and the reset environments live under `formal/`. The
plan names eight targets, T1 to T8, over two formal tops: `dtp` for the
JTAG-domain blocks and `cross_trigger_network` for the single-clock
cross-trigger logic. Every target row names the traceability rows it
corroborates, the item `run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal` launches, and the
part of the block the open path checks. The `dtp` blocks share
`formal/fpv/sby/dtp.sby`, whose task tags select one block's property module;
`dtp_cross_trigger_network.sby` beside it carries the other top, and each top
has its own environment module.

[[dtp-formal-targets]]
==== Targets

A target is a described block whose behaviour is a boolean over a small
control state: a state machine, a handshake, a gating term, a register with a
fixed access policy. Data movement is out (<<dtp-formal-exclusions>>).

[cols="1,3,3,2,2",options="header",]
|===
|Target |Described block |Rows |Formal top and item |Path
|T1 TAP controller |`jtag_tap_ctrlr`: the sixteen-state Moore machine and the scan-control flags |F1.1, F1.2 |`dtp`, `dtp_tap_fpv` |Open
|T2 Instruction register and TDR selection |`jtag_inst_reg` (6-bit shift, 64-bit one-hot decode, IDCODE on reset) and the PTAP select and TDO multiplexer terms: bypass for undefined opcodes, one TDR selected per instruction, zero-length bypass, the read-only IDCODE and JTAG_CAPS captures |F1.3, F1.4, F1.5 (selection), F2.5 |`dtp`, `dtp_ir_fpv` |Open for the opcodes up to `0x1E` (<<dtp-formal-observations>>)
|T3 Reset hierarchy and persistence |`jtag_trst_n = trst_n AND pwr_on_rst_ni`, `rst_n = !test_logic_reset`, `chrst_n` under `jtag_tmp`; `jtag_tmp_status_reg`; `jtag_ic_reset_reg` with `reset_hold`; `jtag_3dcr_reg` with `config_hold` |F1.2, F1.6, F2.1, F2.2 |`dtp`, `dtp_reset_fpv` |Open
|T4 STAP selection and debug disable |The PTAP 3DCR select, the four `jtag_stap` instances (I/O, SMC, SEP, extra) with their 3DCR and lockup flops, the `stap_host` return path in `jtag_intf_unit`, the two-flop `dbg_disable_i` synchronizers |F3.3, F3.4, F3.5, F3.6 |`dtp`, `dtp_stap_fpv` |Open
|T5 iJTAG SIB network and debug disable |The three `prim_jtag_sib_mux_post` instances (DFT secure, DFT non-secure, DFD) |F3.1, F3.2, F3.6 |`dtp`, `dtp_sib_fpv` |Open
|T6 Debug control and clock stop |`jtag_debug_ctrl_reg` with its `cla_clock_stop` synchronizer; `ctn_clock_stop_ctrl` |F2.3, F2.4, F5.7 |`dtp`, `dtp_dbgctl_fpv`; `cross_trigger_network`, `dtp_ctn_clkstop_fpv` |Open
|T7 Cross-trigger port and matrix |`ctp_handshake_ctrl` (sender and receiver), `cross_trigger_port_core` mode multiplexers and wire-OR edge detect, `ctp_pulse_stretcher`, one `ctm_src_selector` slice |F5.1, F5.2, F5.3, F5.6 |`cross_trigger_network`, `dtp_ctn_xtrig_fpv` |Open
|T8 Cross-trigger CSR access |The CTN address map into the AXI-Lite crossbar and its error subordinate; the generated register blocks' field access, strobes and reset values |F5.4, F5.5 |`cross_trigger_network`, `dtp_ctn_csr_fpv` |Open for the address-map contract and the field policy of one CTP and one CTM slice
|===

[[dtp-formal-exclusions]]
==== Exclusions

[cols="3,4",options="header",]
|===
|Logic |Reason and where the credit lives
|The JTAG2AXI bridges: the DTMCS and DMI registers and the vendored `tdr2axi` Debug Module and SBA engine |No property module binds to them; the scenarios of Test Procedures section 2 check the DTMCS and DMI registers, the sticky DMI status, System Bus Access writes and reads, and their errors in simulation, and T2 checks the PTAP selection of the OTP bridges' DTMCS and DMI registers.
|Boundary-scan data and the external iJTAG instrument chains |Loopbacks in `tb_top`; the design carries selection and gating only, which T2 and T5 cover.
|`ctp_pulse_stretcher` at large `STRETCH_MULT` |A 16-bit down counter, up to 65 536 cycles. The T7 proofs assume `STRETCH_MULT <= 4`; the counter's reload and terminal behaviour is one inductive property of its own.
|The full 26 x 26 cross-trigger matrix |A generate of identical selector slices; one slice with a free select mask stands for the array.
|The AXI-Lite crossbar internals (demux address-write lock, per-port FIFOs) |Vendor IP; T8 checks the address-map contract at the CTN port and the register blocks behind it.
|The pad-ring wire-OR bus |The shared wire and its pull live outside the RTL; the receive polarity is the port's own and T7 states it on the synchronized pad input. A property over several chiplets on one wire needs an environment model of the bus and is not a DUT property.
|TDO retiming and the lockup flops |Negedge flops are ordinary state under `multiclock on`; the timing margin they buy is not a property.
|Deriving `dbg_disable_t`, the adopter-defined layout of the IC_RESET external slice, real downstream TAPs |Integration level, as for the simulation bench.
|===

[[dtp-formal-properties]]
==== Properties by target

Every label follows the style chapter: `ast_` assertions, `asm_` assumptions,
`cov_` covers, each a boolean over current and `$past` values. Priority P0
marks the properties that need an unbounded proof (`prove`) before any
release; P1 and P2 sign off on a bounded result at the target's depth
(<<dtp-formal-depths>>). The P0 set is the security-gating set plus the reset
hierarchy: every scan-side disable field gates exactly its path, and no reset
source reaches a register the standard reserves for another.

T1 is the property module in `formal/props/dtp_tap_props.sv`, bound to
`jtag_tap_ctrlr` by `formal/props/dtp_tap_bind.sv`:

[cols="3,3,1,1",options="header",]
|===
|Label |Checks |Row |Prio
|`ast_tap_state_one_hot` |The state register holds exactly one state |F1.1 |P0
|`ast_tap_five_tms_high_reach_tlr` |Five consecutive TMS-high cycles reach Test-Logic-Reset from any state |F1.2 |P0
|`ast_tap_tlr_holds_on_tms_high`, `ast_tap_tlr_exits_to_rti`, `ast_tap_rti_holds_on_tms_low`, `ast_tap_shift_holds_on_tms_low`, `ast_tap_update_dr_only_from_exit`, `ast_tap_update_ir_only_from_exit` |The legal TMS arcs into and out of the reset, idle, shift and update states |F1.1 |P1
|`ast_tap_select_exclusive`, `ast_tap_moore_flags_follow_state`, `ast_tap_negedge_flags_follow_state` |The DR and IR selects are exclusive; the Moore flags and the negedge-clocked scan-control flags follow the state |F1.1 |P1
|===

The T1 item also binds the shared JTAG checker to `dtp` on the primary TAP
(`formal/props/dtp_jtag_fv_bind.sv`, `hw/common/dv/vip/ocah_jtag_vip/sva/ocah_jtag_fv.sv`),
whose slave rules it asserts: `ast_jtag_state_onehot`,
`ast_jtag_state_next_legal` (every transition follows the IEEE 1149.1
controller diagram), `ast_jtag_trst_tlr`, `ast_jtag_tlr_tms5`,
`ast_jtag_tdo_oen_shift_only` and `ast_jtag_tdo_stable_while_tck_high` (the
enable, and TDO while the enable is active, change on the falling edge alone),
with the covers `cov_jtag_shift_dr`, `cov_jtag_shift_ir`,
`cov_jtag_tlr_from_five_tms_high` and `cov_jtag_tdo_driven`. The T8 item
binds the shared AXI checker
(`hw/common/dv/vip/ocah_axi_vip/sva/ocah_axi_fv.sv`) with the design's side
asserted: on `cross_trigger_network` the slave rules
`ast_axi_<ch>_valid_reset_low`, `ast_axi_<ch>_valid_held` and
`ast_axi_<ch>_payload_stable` for the B and R channels,
`ast_axi_b_not_before_aw`, `ast_axi_r_not_before_ar`,
`ast_axi_axil_b_resp_legal` and `ast_axi_axil_r_resp_legal`, with the five
`cov_axi_<ch>_handshake` covers (<<dtp-formal-assumptions>>). Every report
names them under the bind instance, for example
`u_ocah_axi_fv.gen_axi_b_valid_held.ast_axi_b_valid_held`.

The remaining targets, one property module per block under
`formal/props/`, with the intents the labels carry. T2 and T3 bind to
`jtag_ptap`, T4 and T5 to `jtag_intf_unit` with the four STAPs and the three
SIBs packed into per-instance vectors, T6 to `jtag_debug_ctrl_reg` and
`ctn_clock_stop_ctrl`, and T7 and T8 to `cross_trigger_network` with
external port 0 and matrix slice 0 standing for their arrays:

[cols="1,3,4,1",options="header",]
|===
|Target |Module |Property intents |Prio
|T2 |`dtp_ir_props.sv` on `jtag_inst_reg` and the PTAP select terms |`ast_ir_decoded_one_hot`: the decoded instruction is one-hot. `ast_ir_capture_loads_01`: Capture-IR loads `6'b000001`. `ast_ir_update_decodes_shift_reg`: after Update-IR the decoded instruction is `1 << shift_reg`. `ast_ir_reset_is_idcode`: Test-Logic-Reset, TRST and power-on reset leave IDCODE decoded. `ast_tdr_select_one_hot`: at most one register select is high in a DR scan, each select equals the DR select under its own instruction, the bypass register is also clocked by IDCODE, INV_BYPASS and SELECT_IJTAG scans and by ZERO_LENGTH_BYPASS scans with the PTAP 3DCR select set, and the TDO multiplexer reads the register of the instruction. `ast_undefined_selects_bypass`: every opcode outside the defined set selects the bypass register. `ast_zlb_bypasses_retimer`: under ZERO_LENGTH_BYPASS in Shift-DR with the PTAP 3DCR select clear, TDO follows TDI in the same step; with the select set the bypass register is selected as under BYPASS. `ast_idcode_caps_read_only`: the IDCODE and JTAG_CAPS captures load the parameter constants. Covers: each of the 31 opcodes from `0x00` to `0x1E` decoded, the bypass selection for the undefined opcode `0x0F` and for the RISC-V reserved block, a read of the JTAG_CAPS TDR and of the SMC OTP DTMCS TDR, a DEBUG_CONTROL shift. The module runs with every optional feature of the DTP configuration enabled, and the ir tasks load the opcodes up to `0x1E` alone, so every clause over an instruction from `0x1F` up goes unchecked on the open path: `ast_zlb_bypasses_retimer`, `ast_undefined_selects_bypass` for `0x21` to `0x3C`, and the `ast_ir_update_decodes_shift_reg` and `ast_tdr_select_one_hot` terms for the SMC fabric bridge's DTMCS and DMI instructions `0x1F` and `0x20`, ZERO_LENGTH_BYPASS, INV_BYPASS and BYPASS (<<dtp-formal-observations>>). |P1; the decode one-hot and the undefined-to-bypass rule P0
|T3 |`dtp_reset_props.sv` on the PTAP reset terms, `jtag_tmp`, `jtag_tmp_status_reg`, `jtag_ic_reset_reg`, `jtag_3dcr_reg` |`ast_trst_or_por_resets_tap`: either source low forces Test-Logic-Reset and the IDCODE instruction. `ast_tlr_asserts_rst_n`: `rst_n` is low exactly while the negedge-sampled state is Test-Logic-Reset. `ast_chrst_follows_rst_unless_persistent`: `chrst_n` equals `rst_n` while persistence is off and stays high in Test-Logic-Reset while it is on. `ast_tmp_on_only_by_clamp_hold`, `ast_tmp_off_by_release_or_escape`: the two TMP transitions and no other; the bypass escape needs Update-IR, an explicit BYPASS opcode and the escape bit. `ast_tmp_survives_tlr`: Test-Logic-Reset leaves the TMP state unchanged. `ast_ic_reset_hold_blocks_tlr`: with `reset_hold` 0, Test-Logic-Reset leaves `reset_enable` and `reset_control` unchanged; with `reset_hold` 1 it restores them to all-ones. `ast_ic_reset_trst_clears_all`: TRST restores every IC_RESET bit including `reset_hold`. `ast_ic_reset_ovrd_is_not_enable`: `.ovrd` is the inversion of `reset_enable` and `.val` equals `reset_control`. `ast_3dcr_config_hold_blocks_tlr`: the PTAP 3DCR survives Test-Logic-Reset while `config_hold` is set and clears on TRST. Covers: TRST alone and the power-on reset alone asserted, persistence on then off by each exit, an IC_RESET override held across a Test-Logic-Reset, a 3DCR held across a Test-Logic-Reset. |P0
|T4 |`dtp_stap_props.sv` on the PTAP 3DCR, each `jtag_stap`, and the `stap_host` terms of `jtag_intf_unit` |`ast_stap_disable_forces_sel_low`, `ast_stap_disable_tdo_low`, `ast_stap_disable_blocks_3dcr_update`: with the STAP's disable field high, `stap_sel` is 0, `host_tdo_o` is 0, and the 3DCR update register holds its value. `ast_stap_unselected_parks_tms`: with `stap_sel` 0, `host_tap_ctrl_o.tms` equals `tms_hold`; with 1 it follows the client TMS. `ast_stap_tdo_oen_only_when_selected_shifting`: `host_tdo_oen_o` is `stap_sel AND shift_en`. `ast_stap_host_disable_loops_back`: with `stap_host` disabled the PTAP scan input is the chain's own output and every host scan-control enable is 0. `ast_ptap_stap_select_routes_tdo`: with the PTAP 3DCR select set, TDO comes from the STAP chain, else from the TDR multiplexer. `ast_stap_chain_quiet_unselected`: with the PTAP 3DCR select clear, the select, capture, shift and update enables of the STAP chain's scan control are 0. `ast_dbg_disable_sync_fail_closed`: each synchronizer resets to 1 and follows its input after two `tck` edges. Covers: the I/O, SMC, SEP and extra STAP each selected; a STAP disabled while selected; the return path disabled during a shift, and enabled during a shift with the PTAP select set. |P0
|T5 |`dtp_sib_props.sv` on the three SIBs |`ast_sib_disable_forces_en_low`, `ast_sib_disable_blocks_update`, `ast_sib_disable_host_quiet`: with the SIB's field high, the segment enable is 0, the SIB bit does not update, and the host `capture_en`, `shift_en`, `update_en`, `select` and `host_scan_out_o` are 0. `ast_sib_open_routes_host`: with the enable set, the client scan output is the host scan input, else the SIB's own bit. `ast_sib_host_ctrl_follows_client`: the host enables equal the client enables AND `select` while enabled. `ast_sib_chain_order`: the DFD SIB's input is the non-secure SIB's output, whose input is the secure SIB's output. Covers: every SIB pattern (eight), each SIB disabled while open. |P0
|T6 |`dtp_dbgctl_props.sv` on `jtag_debug_ctrl_reg`; `dtp_ctn_clkstop_props.sv` on `ctn_clock_stop_ctrl` |`ast_dbgctl_rw_bits_hold`: bits 3:0 change only on an Update-DR of the DEBUG_CONTROL TDR. `ast_dbgctl_ro_bit_captures_status`: bit 4 captures the synchronized `cla_clock_stop` and ignores the shifted value. `ast_dbgctl_outputs_follow_update_reg`: the four outputs equal the update register bits. `ast_clkstop_or_after_three`: `stop_clks_o` equals the OR of `jtag_clock_stop_i` and every `clk_stop_req_i` bit sampled three `clk_i` cycles earlier. `ast_cla_status_excludes_jtag`: `cla_clock_stop_o` is the OR of the request bits alone, with no register. `ast_clkstop_low_in_reset`: `stop_clks_o` is 0 while `rst_ni` is low. Covers: a JTAG stop with no CLA request, a CLA request with no JTAG stop, the boot-stall override with each stall value. |P1
|T7 |`dtp_ctn_xtrig_props.sv` on `ctp_handshake_ctrl`, `cross_trigger_port_core`, `ctp_pulse_stretcher`, `ctm_src_selector` |`ast_hs_sender_states`, `ast_hs_receiver_states`: the two machines hold legal states and take only the four-phase arcs, as functions of the controller's own inputs. `ast_hs_req_stable_until_ack`: `ct_req_out` holds until the synchronized ack. `ast_hs_ack_mirrors_req`: the receiver asserts ack on a synchronized request and drops it when the request drops. `ast_hs_dst_is_one_pulse`: `ct_dst` rises only from the idle receiver on a synchronized request and lasts one cycle. `ast_hs_busy_lags_state`: `busy` is the registered not-idle of either machine. `ast_hs_reset_recovers_sender`: `reset_i` returns the sender to IDLE with `ct_req_out` low, and leaves the receiver untouched. `ast_ctp_mode_enables_exclusive`: the wire-OR and point-to-point pad enables are mutually exclusive and match `CONFIG.MODE`, and wire-OR mode masks the handshake's source and holds its sender in reset. `ast_ctp_p2p_inputs_gated`: the controller sees the synchronized request and acknowledge ANDed with the registered point-to-point input enable of two cycles earlier, so they read low until the pads have been enabled that long. `ast_ctp_wire_or_sense`: the optional inversion sits after the synchronizer, so the sensed wire rests at 1 in either sense. `ast_ctp_wire_or_edge_pulse`: in wire-OR mode `ct_dst` is the sensed wire having moved from rest to asserted one cycle earlier, the falling edge of the raw pad for INVERT=0 and its rising edge for INVERT=1, and the release edge yields nothing; in point-to-point mode it is the handshake's `ct_dst`. `ast_stretch_width`: a trigger holds the output for `STRETCH_MULT + 1` cycles and a new trigger reloads the counter. `ast_ctm_slice_route`: `ct_src_o` equals the registered OR of `ct_dst AND select`, and an all-zero select isolates the output. `ast_ctp_status_reflects_pins`: `STATUS.REQ_IN` and `ACK_IN` equal the synchronized de-inverted pad values, `REQ_OUT` and `ACK_OUT` the de-inverted registered outputs, and `BUSY` the registered busy of the active mode, the stretched pulse in wire-OR mode and the handshake's busy otherwise; in wire-OR mode `REQ_OUT` and `ACK_OUT` read 0. Covers: a full four-phase cycle against the peer model, a synchronized request held off by the input gate, a stretched wire-OR pulse of the maximum assumed width, a wire-OR receive in each sense, a route from an internal source to an external port and back, reset in each mode. |P1
|T8 |`dtp_ctn_csr_props.sv` on `cross_trigger_network` and one CTP and one CTM register block |`ast_csr_unmapped_decerr`: an address outside the matrix register extent (`0x000` to `0x0CF`) and the port windows (`0x200` to `0x2FF`) completes with DECERR on both channels, including `0x0D0` to `0x1FF` of the matrix aperture; every byte address inside them completes with OKAY. `ast_csr_ctp_window`: an address in `0x200 + 16n` to `0x20F + 16n` reaches CTP `n` alone, and a crossbar master port carries a request only while the subordinate port holds it accepted and unanswered, from the cycle after its address handshake, and never again once a master port has released it. `ast_csr_ctm_readback`: the matrix block dispatches the word address it receives, its readback follows address bits 7:2, and the response carries the dispatched readback. `ast_csr_reset_values`: CONFIG, STRETCH_MULT and every CT_DST_SELECT read 0 after reset. `ast_csr_status_write_ignored`: a write to STATUS returns OKAY and changes nothing. `ast_csr_write_reaches_regblock`: a write that the port 0 register block dispatches carries the word address of the AW and the data of the W the subordinate port accepted, with the W strobes expanded to the bit-enable. `ast_csr_strobe_masks_bytes`: a write changes only the strobed bytes. `ast_csr_read_after_write`: a read after a completed write returns the written field value. Covers: a DECERR write and read, a DECERR read past the matrix register extent, a CTP and a CTM read-after-write, a masked write, a write and a read that a master port has released before the subordinate port answers them. |P1
|===

[[dtp-formal-observations]]
==== RTL behaviour the properties pin

These properties pin the following behaviour:

* `stop_clks_o` is the OR of the requests through a two-flop synchronizer
  and an output flop, three `clk_i` cycles (`ast_clkstop_or_after_three`).
* `INT_CT_MODE[i] = 0` is the wire-OR pulse mode,
  `ModeWireOr = ~INT_CT_MODE[i]` (`ast_ctp_mode_enables_exclusive`, checked
  per internal port).
* `CONFIG.RESET` returns only the sender machine to IDLE; the receiver has no
  reset arc (`ast_hs_reset_recovers_sender`).

The proofs and cover traces also show:

* The bypass register's select term shares its default arm with the
  undefined opcodes, so IDCODE, INV_BYPASS and SELECT_IJTAG scans clock the
  bypass register too while TDO reads their own register
  (`ast_tdr_select_one_hot`).
* The TMP status register resets its bypass-escape bit (bit 0) to 0, as the
  example TMP control register of IEEE 1149.1-2013 does, so the escape exit
  is disabled after reset and needs a TMP_STATUS write first; an escape
  enabled by default would let a BYPASS load aimed at another device in the
  chain release CLAMP_HOLD (`cov_tmp_off_by_escape`).
* The instruction decode is `jtag_instruction_decoded_e'(2 ** shift)`. The
  open-path frontend sizes the operand of a type cast on its own, so the
  power is 32 bits wide in the model: opcode `0x1F` decodes to instruction
  bits 31 to 63 at once and every higher opcode to zero, while Verilator
  sizes the operand to the cast type and decodes every opcode. The T1 and T2
  items therefore load the opcodes up to `0x1E` alone
  (`asm_env_ir_open_path_decode_range`, <<dtp-formal-assumptions>>). On the
  open path that leaves every clause over an instruction from `0x1F` up
  unchecked and no cover reaching one (T2 in <<dtp-formal-properties>>).
  The other items on `dtp` carry no bound, so they reach instruction
  bits 31 to 63 only together, through `0x1F`, and never one at a time as
  the RTL decodes them: the T3 bypass escape through an explicit BYPASS load
  (`0x3F`) rests on simulation, while the escape through BYPASS_ALT (`0x00`)
  is decoded as in the RTL. The instruction package's own enum values use
  the shift form `DecodedIrWidth'(1'b1) << opcode`, which every frontend
  sizes alike (`formal-property-style.adoc`, Accepted constructs).

Two paths have no reset of their own, and their X-safety from reset is
outside the open path: `cla_clock_stop_o` is combinational, and the
`cla_clock_stop` synchronizer inside `jtag_debug_ctrl_reg` has its reset tied
high.

[[dtp-formal-assumptions]]
==== Assumptions and environments

Every top has one environment module bound at the top, in the shape of
`formal/fpv/sby/dtp_sby_env.sv`: the asynchronous resets are asserted up to
the first sampling edge, so each clock domain starts from its reset state,
and released there for the rest of the trace
(`asm_env_resets_asserted`, `asm_env_resets_released`). Each property module
adds `asm_initial_reset` from the macro layer on its own reset. An assumption
that only some tasks need sits in a module of its own beside the top's, read
by those tasks only. Beyond that:

* `dtp`: before the first edge TRST and the power-on reset take any values
  with at least one of them low, so either source alone resets the TAP, and
  the system reset is low. The `dbg_disable_i` synchronizers, which only the
  power-on reset clears, start from any value when TRST alone is low, as
  after a power-on reset long past. `asm_env_scan_reset_inactive` holds the
  scan reset high, which the RTL leaves unused, and
  `asm_env_test_mode_inactive` holds DFT test mode off.
  `dbg_disable_i`, `tms`, `tdi` and every host scan input are free, except
  that the return data of the external scan chains and of the downstream
  TAPs holds through the tck-high phase
  (`asm_env_scan_returns_hold_while_tck_high`), the shape of a JTAG slave's
  TDO. The AXI responder ports are free too: the JTAG-domain targets never
  reach them. The T1 item binds the shared JTAG checker `ocah_jtag_fv` on
  the primary TAP (`dtp_jtag_fv_bind.sv`), which assumes the host's phase
  rule, `asm_jtag_tms_tdi_stable_while_tck_high`, and asserts the TAP's rules
  (<<dtp-formal-properties>>). The T1 and T2 items also read
  `dtp_ir_sby_env.sv`, whose `asm_env_ir_open_path_decode_range` bounds the
  opcode an Update-IR loads to `0x1E` for the frontend sizing recorded in
  <<dtp-formal-observations>>: in the model `0x1F` sets instruction bits 31
  to 63 at once, among them the zero-length bypass that routes TDO around its
  retimer, and every higher opcode sets none.
* `cross_trigger_network`: the AXI-Lite manager is the DTP's bridge, so
  `asm_env_axil_one_outstanding` allows one write, its AW and one W beat, and
  one read in flight, and in the T8 item the shared AXI checker bound by
  `dtp_ctn_axi_fv_bind.sv` with its master rules assumed holds each valid with
  its payload until its ready (`asm_axi_aw_valid_held`,
  `asm_axi_w_valid_held`, `asm_axi_ar_valid_held`,
  `asm_axi_aw_payload_stable`, `asm_axi_w_payload_stable`,
  `asm_axi_ar_payload_stable`) and keeps each valid low in reset; the same
  instance asserts the network's responses (<<dtp-formal-properties>>). The
  T6 and T7 items on this top read the port through its covers alone and
  take no handshake assumption. `asm_env_ctp_peer_four_phase` models the far
  end of external port 0 on the synchronized, de-inverted request and
  acknowledge (the far end acknowledges a request it sees and withdraws the
  acknowledge after the request drops, holds its own request until it sees
  the acknowledge, and raises no new one while the acknowledge is high). The
  T7 item alone also reads `dtp_ctn_xtrig_sby_env.sv`, whose
  `asm_env_ctp_stretch_small` bounds port 0's `STRETCH_MULT` to 4; the T6
  and T8 items take the full field. The pad inputs are otherwise free.

Every `cov_` label stays reachable under these assumptions; an unreached
cover fails the item, and an assumption change re-runs every cover of its
top (`formal-property-style.adoc`, cover reachability).

[[dtp-formal-depths]]
==== Tasks and depths

Each block has its own `bmc`, `cover` and `prove` task triple, named
`<block>_bmc`, `<block>_cover` and `<block>_prove`; the T7 item runs the
first two. On the `dtp` top the
triples share `formal/fpv/sby/dtp.sby`, selected by task tags, and one app
table per block passes its triple in `args`, so `testlists/formal.toml` lists
one item per block. The `cross_trigger_network` top has its own task file.
The `prove` task carries no depth; `bmc` and `cover` share
one depth per block, set above the step at which the block's deepest cover
is reached. The table gives the step at which each block's deepest cover
is reachable and the depth set above it; the derivations that follow give
the structural latency of each route.

The bound follows the block's structural latency. Under `multiclock on` every
step is a time point and one `tck` cycle costs two steps; the reset
environment releases the resets at the first `tck` posedge, so a state that is
`n` `tck` edges from Test-Logic-Reset is first observable at step `3 + 2n`.
`clk_i` is a second explicit clock on the `dtp` top and toggles freely, so a
`clk_i`-domain latency of `k` cycles adds steps only where `2k` exceeds the
`tck` path. The `cross_trigger_network` top is single-clock and its depth is
in `clk_i` cycles. Two `tck` edge counts recur: an instruction load from
Test-Logic-Reset is 12 edges (Run-Test/Idle, Select-DR, Select-IR,
Capture-IR, Shift-IR, six shifts with the last leaving to Exit1-IR,
Update-IR), and a DR scan of `b` bits is `b + 4` edges (Select-DR,
Capture-DR, Shift-DR, `b` shifts with the last leaving to Exit1-DR,
Update-DR). Capture-IR loads `000001`, so an opcode whose low `z` bits are
zero, `z` at most five, loads in `6 - z` shifts, `z` edges fewer.

[cols="1,4,1,1",options="header",]
|===
|Target |Deepest cover and the route the trace takes |Step |Depth
|T1 |`cov_tap_tlr_from_five_tms_high`: Run-Test/Idle, Select-DR, Capture-DR, then five TMS-high edges into Test-Logic-Reset |19 |30
|T2 |`cov_undefined_opcode_bypass`: the undefined opcode `0x0F` loaded, then a DR scan reaches Shift-DR through the bypass register; the thirty-one opcode covers each load one instruction |33 |40
|T3 |`cov_tmp_off_by_escape`: TMP_STATUS loaded and its escape bit written, CLAMP_HOLD loaded, BYPASS_ALT loaded with one shift, persistence off at the edge after Update-IR |67 |75
|T4 |`cov_stap_disabled_while_selected` on the extra STAP: TAP_3DCR loaded and the PTAP 3DCR select set in a scan of its own, one chain scan opens every SIB and a second sets every `stap_sel`, then the synchronized disable rises |67 |75
|T5 |`cov_sib_disabled_while_open` on the DFD SIB and the eight SIB patterns: SELECT_IJTAG or RUNBIST loaded in five IR shifts, a 3-bit DR scan writes the SIBs, and the synchronized disable is high at the edge into Run-Test/Idle that follows |41 |55
|T6 |`cov_dbgctl_boot_stall_ovrd_stall_high`: DEBUG_CONTROL (`0x18`) loaded in three IR shifts, then a 4-bit DR scan moves the captured `cla_clock_stop` bit into bit 0 and the first TDI bit into bit 1 |37 |55 on `dtp`; the `dtp_ctn_clkstop_fpv` covers are reached at step 6 and its triple uses depth 10
|T7 |`cov_hs_four_phase_complete`: a CSR write sets the mode, a source pulse starts the sender, the peer acknowledges and releases |18 |20; the item runs `bmc` and `cover` alone, since the block is P1 and signs off on a bounded result
|T8 |`cov_csr_ctp_read_after_write`: a write to CONFIG and a read of it complete through the crossbar and the register block |8 |12; the `prove` task also carries the shared AXI checker's assertions on the port
|===

A cover added at a greater distance raises its block's depth. The
derivations behind the table: a six-shift instruction load from
Test-Logic-Reset is 12 `tck` edges and a DR scan of `b` bits is `b + 4`, so
the T2 route above costs 15 edges, the load and three edges into Shift-DR,
and the T5 route 19, a five-shift load of 11, a 3-bit scan of 7 and the edge
into Run-Test/Idle; the T3 and T4 routes cost 32 edges, the T4 one because
the PTAP 3DCR select must be set before a chain scan reaches a SIB, and the
T6 route 17, a three-shift load of 9 and a 4-bit scan of 8; the CTN routes
are single-clock cycle counts. The T1
depth also follows a diameter-plus-reset argument: depth 30 is thirteen
`tck` edges after reset release, the state-graph diameter of seven plus a
five-edge TMS reset sequence with one edge of margin.

[[dtp-formal-apps]]
==== App checks

[cols="3,2,5",options="header",]
|===
|Check |Targets |What it establishes
|Property checking (`fpv`) |T1 to T8 as listed |The properties above, with `prove` for the P0 set and bounded `bmc` plus `cover` at the block depth.
|Security-path checks for the debug-disable gating, the P0 set of T4 and T5 |T4, T5 |With a disable field high, its path's select, TDO and host enables are quiet; unbounded by `prove`.
|===

The open path does not establish the static connectivity of `dtp` beyond the
SIB chain order of T5, the access policy of every CSR field from the
SystemRDL beyond the one CTP and the CTM slice of T8, X-safety from reset, or
the unreachability of the code-coverage points the simulation regression
leaves uncovered. A reader who needs them runs a tool for each through the
runner's site layer (`run-dv.adoc`, Licensed backends and the companion
boundary); `DTP_FCOV.adoc` (Unreachability exclusion list) describes the
coverage exclusion list such an analysis writes.

[[dtp-formal-running]]
==== Running and grading

[source,bash]
----
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal                    # the smoke group: dtp_tap_fpv
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal --items fpv        # every formal item
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal --items dtp_stap_fpv
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal --dry-run          # the rendered sby command
----

The `flist` stage regenerates `formal/build/dtp.f` from Bender, the filelist
both task files read; the `formal` stage runs one item's `bmc`, `cover`
and `prove` tasks in one `sby` invocation whose work directories are
`<run>/stages/formal/<item>_<task>/`, for example
`dtp_tap_fpv_tap_bmc`. The `smoke` group holds `dtp_tap_fpv`; the `fpv` group
holds every item: `dtp_tap_fpv`, `dtp_ir_fpv`, `dtp_reset_fpv`,
`dtp_stap_fpv`, `dtp_sib_fpv` and `dtp_dbgctl_fpv` on the `dtp` top, and
`dtp_ctn_clkstop_fpv`, `dtp_ctn_xtrig_fpv` and `dtp_ctn_csr_fpv` on
`cross_trigger_network`. The
runner grades an item from the task status files and JUnit reports, never
from the exit code: every task `PASS` with every cover reached is `PASS`; a
counterexample, an induction failure or an unreached cover is `FAIL` in the
`formal_fail` bucket, and `result.json` carries the `formal` object with the
proven and covered counts (`run-dv.adoc`, Formal result grading).

The FAIL path is checked by inverting one assertion body, for example
`dr_select_i == ir_select_i` in `ast_tap_select_exclusive`, and running the
same command: the `bmc` task reports `FAIL` with the counterexample trace at
`stages/formal/dtp_tap_fpv_tap_bmc/engine_0/trace.vcd`, the `prove` task
reports `FAIL`, and the item is `FAIL`. Check each item's FAIL path the same
way: invert one assertion body, rerun, and confirm the item reports `FAIL`.

The open path needs a yosys-slang build with the concurrent-assertion
lowering and an SMT solver beside `sby` (`formal-property-style.adoc`,
Open-path toolchain); the runner guide's Formal verification chapter covers
the local and container runs and the licensed backends. Formal mode is a
developer-host flow: hosted CI runs no formal job.

[[dtp-formal-dependencies]]
==== Dependencies between targets

T3 and T4 hold the P0 set and stand alone on the `dtp` top. T8 takes the
AXI-Lite handshake rules from the shared checker `ocah_axi_fv`, bound with
the environment's side assumed, and T1 takes the host's phase
rule from `ocah_jtag_fv`; the point-to-point peer model stays in the
`cross_trigger_network` environment, which T7 needs. A target is complete
when its item passes with every cover reached and its FAIL path is checked.

=== Regression Groups

The simulation groups below come from `testlists/all.toml` and the per-area lists it includes;
`testlists/formal.toml` carries the formal item `dtp_tap_fpv` in its own
`smoke` group (<<dtp-formal-plan>>).

[cols=",,",options="header",]
|===
|Group |Tests |Purpose
|`smoke` |`dtp_sanity_test`, `dtp_jtag_idcode_test`, `dtp_jtag_tmp_status_register_smoke_test`, `dtp_ijtag_sib_all_on_test`, `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_single_write_read_test`, `dtp_xtrig_wire_or_test` |Fast gate: one basic scenario per feature area
|`functional` |`dtp_sanity_test`, `dtp_jtag_idcode_test`, and `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_single_write_read_test` |Fast functional regression
|`basic_jtag` |The Smoke and Basic JTAG scenarios |TAP, instruction, reset, BSR-loopback, boundary-scan-disabled, and TMP smoke coverage
|`debug_tdr` |The TMP_STATUS, IC_RESET, DEBUG_CONTROL, and JTAG_CAPS tests |Debug TDR coverage
|`all` |Every scenario-expanded test (`expected_count`-pinned in `testlists/all.toml`) |Full OCAH DTP regression
|`jtag2axi` |The System Bus Access write and read-back scenario of each bridge, and the DTMCS and DMI status, Debug Module activation, System Bus Access size, System Bus Access error, and back-pressure scenarios of all three |AXI/AXI-Lite bridge, DMI status, Debug Module activation, access size, access error, and port timing coverage
|`scan` |STAP, 3DCR, and iJTAG scenarios |IEEE 1838 and IEEE 1687 coverage
|`dbg_disable` |The scan debug-disable matrix test plus every scan-side directed gating test |Per-field scan-side `dbg_disable_t` closure (16-cell coverage contract)
|`xtrig` |XTRIG, CTP, and CTM scenarios |Cross-trigger and CSR coverage
|===

[[dtp-vplan-known-limitations]]
=== Known Limitations

Each row names the level that owns the behaviour the bench cannot reach
(<<dtp-vplan-scope>>); a PASS here closes the reachable clauses of an entry only.

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Limitation |Impact
|iJTAG scan chains end in the bench's 4-, 5-, and 6-bit instrument stubs in `tb_top` |iJTAG scenarios validate the DTP-side SIB plumbing, chain length, and stub capture/update, not an external instrument network (integration level)
|The downstream STAP TAPs are behavioral `ocah_jtag_vip` devices (5-bit IR, IDCODE, one writable TDR) attached only by the STAP-selection scenarios and the zero-length-bypass scenario |Other scan scenarios see the STAP host ports as wire loopbacks; the real SMC and SEP debug TAPs are integration level
|The extended STAP host scan interface ends in the bench's 7-bit host segment in `tb_top`, placed only by the extended-STAP scenario |Other scenarios see the host scan as a wire loopback; the external STAP chain is integration level
|The JTAG2AXI bridge ports end in bench responders that map every address |Fabric address decode and the default subordinate are SMC-level
|The DUT is elaborated from the bench configuration table, or for 0.19 from that table with the boundary-scan enables clear, and the DFT ports are driven inactive |Entries are closed for the bench configuration, and 0.19 also for its boundary-scan-disabled variant; other DTP parameterizations are integration level, and DFT-mode behaviour is scan-insertion level
|The bring-up startup vector clears all debug disables |Gating behavior is only observable under explicit disable stimulus; the `dbg_disable` group drives directed and seeded disable vectors and closes the 16-cell scan-side coverage contract
|===
